**"Elder" Amunet:** I've taught you and cared for you as my own daughter all this time... because of this blessing that's been with you since your birth. I know no one else in the world would understand the weight of life and death more than you do.
**"Elder" Amunet:** In the eyes of an Aidonian, "Death" is the end of all life, a destination we will all eventually reach. Humans should not resist it, but learn how to face it.
**"Elder" Amunet:** You can only live your life to the fullest after understanding this principle.
**"Elder" Amunet:** And when you do, even if you eventually leave Aidonia's protection... even if you must carry the curse forever... you can live with your head held high, like everyone else.
**Castorice:** No, how can I—
**"Elder" Amunet:** Come now. It's time. I have already taught you all that I know...
**"Elder" Amunet:** Show me that you are a fitting Holy Maiden... or at least, that you possess the resolve to become one.

*(After the battle)*
**Castorice:** ...
**Castorice:** This was the last time I followed her command. I gently touched the elderly woman before me.
**Castorice:** The executioner who had seen too much death died the same way as everyone else... A gasp for air, a wistful look in her eyes, and a barely perceptible tremor in her fingertips.
**Castorice:** Elder Amunet, the person who tried to teach me about "Death," ultimately vanished like dew in the morning mist. She spent her life embodying the beliefs of the Aidonians, and when death finally came, she embraced it exactly as their doctrine had taught...
**Castorice:** I have nothing but respect for her.
**Castorice:** I erected a cenotaph in her honor in the marshes outside Aidonia's city walls with the others I had laid to rest. Then, I set out once more in search of Thanatos.
**Castorice:** It was a long, long journey... I heard so many discussions about death along the way. "Life finds meaning in death." "The world improves because there is an end"...
**Castorice:** It seems the most terrible darkness in this world. Everyone talks about its heavy weight. Some resist it, some worship it, and some even revere it...
**Castorice:** How did a disappeared Titan become the deepest fear of humankind...
**Castorice:** ...And why do they haunt my dreams?
**Castorice:** I continued my travels to seek an answer. Initially, I was running away. Then, I started searching for it. And now, at the end...
**Castorice:** I just want to know if my touch... if my embrace... can do more than taking things away...
**Castorice:** Can I make anything...? Can I leave anything behind?

**Castorice:** On the day that Oronyx's Trial ended, you must have had many questions, (Trailblazer).
**Castorice:** I felt just as confused. Why, as someone from beyond the sky, were you so cruelly dragged into Amphoreus's fate?
**Castorice:** ...I wished I could take action, but no matter how hard I try, I cannot simply interact with the living as ordinary people do.
**Castorice:** Even when we brushed against each other unintentionally in the Grove... and it was obvious that nothing had happened...
**Castorice:** When an almost unattainable hope finally welled up in my heart...
**Castorice:** When I thought that perhaps my touch would no longer bring sorrow...
**Castorice:** Fate declared to me: It wasn't that the curse of "Death" had finally been lifted...
**Castorice:** ...but that your soul no longer belonged to this world... and there was nothing left for my hands to take. Nothing.
**Castorice:** And did I really think this time would be any different? It's not that I don't understand this reality — just that I'm unwilling to accept it.
**Castorice:** Having walked such a long road, I am still unable to find the answer to life and death. I am resigned to eternal despair...
**Castorice:** Anything I wish to hold on to, "Death" will inevitably wrest from my grasp...
**Castorice:** And from that moment on, I lacked the courage to confirm it again... Until Oronyx laid bare the cruel truth before us.

> **Castorice:** This is the land of their birth... Or perhaps of their death.

> *(Trailblazer)* How would we find them?

> **Castorice:** This alchemy, considered a sacrilegious act, might actually make the souls of mortals and Titans attract each other...
> **Castorice:** Even though Thanatos has been missing for a thousand years and even their followers haven't been able to find them... perhaps that special attraction could summon them here.

> **Castorice:** Indeed. Even one as erudite as Anaxa could only point us to this place, and no further. We will be entering the unknown as soon as we take our first steps... But that won't be the first time in my long journeys.
> **Castorice:** I believe that...

> **Centurion:** Th—This cannot stand! Newcomers, hurry up and go take your positions!

> **Castorice:** ...

### Check the Souls Remains

*(Styxia, the City-State of Dragons and Waves... At present, the River of Souls has encircled this pitiful city. The spirits of Anaxagoras repeatedly recount the past. They seem to be fallen who lived during Era Chrysea. The remains left by these spirits seem to have a connection with alchemy and Thanatos.)*

*(Listen to the souls remains)*
**"Aglaea":** Well, then. Why should I place my trust in you...
**"Aglaea":** Holy maiden from afar?
**Castorice:** W—Wait, this is...
**"Castorice":** I don't quite understand what you're trying to say...
**"Aglaea":** Since the oracle has already pronounced your name as Chrysos Heir of "Death," Okhema ought to treat you as an esteemed guest.
**"Aglaea":** However, you seem to have no intentions of concealing your involvement with the Council of Elders... While most likely being aware of my issues against them.
**"Castorice":** I cannot deny your claim. It was the Council that offered this room to me, and they had only one request... They want the curse of "Death" to be completely subjugated to your will.
**"Castorice":** I... was agreeable to that request.
**"Aglaea":** But why?
**"Castorice":** I am... unable to rid myself of this innate curse. However, from this day onward, I shall use it according to your will. I will never make any superfluous moves on my own.
**"Aglaea":** I see. So that is how they plan to control the Flame-Chase Journey...
**"Aglaea":** They have shifted the heavy responsibility of granting death over to me. If I ever become obsessed with executing people, they will then have ample reason to terminate the Chrysos Heirs' journey.
**"Castorice":** And yet, you seem to... not mind? This is quite surprising to me.
**"Aglaea":** That's because... the fate that we were born with is a burden far heavier than that of an individual's life.
**"Aglaea":** I've woven many unforeseen events into the tapestry of fate. This is but one instance.
**"Aglaea":** Tidy up your precious garments and come with me. Okhema welcomes you, Castorice.

*(You seem to hear a sigh of contentment, as if it comes from the mouth of some colossal being.)*
*(Then, the scenes of the past abruptly dissipated...)*

**Mem:** I didn't think that we'd find vestiges of memory here... Maybe they've got something to do with Oronyx's rune?
**Castorice:** ...Perhaps, but I do not need a reminder from them. This memory is one that I will never forget.

> *(Trailblazer)* So it seems that the Council of Elders has manipulated you once already.
> **Castorice:** Indeed. While my relationship with the Council has always been quite ambiguous, Lady Aglaea has never taken any precautions against me. Instead... She's shown me nothing but complete trust.
> *(Trailblazer)* Aglaea trusts you unreservedly.
> **Castorice:** I... really want to repay her for that trust.
> **Castorice:** But... why would this memory appear right now? Is this really Oronyx's power?

### Continue Exploring Dragonbone City and Speak with the Spirit of the Court Scholar

*(Styxia, the City-State of Dragons and Waves... At present, the River of Souls has encircled this pitiful city. The spirits of Anaxagoras repeatedly recount the past. They seem to be those who fell during Era Chrysea.)*

*(Put back the rune near the double doors)*
**Castorice:** You seem to be able to discern both the past and the future...

*(Listen to the Aidonian Spirit, optional)*
**Aidonian Spirit of the Dead:** Ah... Why isn't there a god yelling in the distance...
**Aidonian Spirit of the Dead:** "Indeed, my downtrodden fellow... Your sorrow and joy are but means for me to amuse myself."
**Aidonian Spirit of the Dead:** I will at least derive some comfort... If all the tears that I had shed were determined by some mightier being above.
**Castorice:** It's... you people...

*(Go through the door)*
**Mem:** This place... must have been a beautiful courtyard in the past.

*(Approach the Court Scholar)*
**Court Scholar:** Please excuse me. I am but a scholar from a distant land who had the misfortune of falling into the River of Souls and turned into a member of the dead...
**Mem:** Huh? This one... seems to know that she's already dead.
**Court Scholar:** Oh, I was merely speculating. But it seems I was right.
**Court Scholar:** And you... You... I recognize you. You're the Holy Maiden of "Death" from Aidonia, right?
**Castorice:** I'm sorry... I cannot bring you back to life. However, should you desire it, I can end your prolonged limbo.
**Court Scholar:** Hah, I'm well aware of that. Even if you were able to revive me, I would never choose that option.
**Court Scholar:** That's because I didn't fall into the River of Souls by accident. Instead, I leaped into it to seek my death.
**Castorice:** ...Why?
**Court Scholar:** If you could see the life I've led, then you would not reproach me so. My life... was far too miserable.
**Court Scholar:** But I did not expect that things stay the same even in death! What a joke! In escaping my mortal life, I've entered a millennium of pain instead.
**Court Scholar:** Cerces above! What cowards humans are, for we fear both life and death simultaneously?
**Castorice:** I'm sorry... I'm also powerless to answer that question.
**Court Scholar:** That's alright, Holy Maiden. At least you are able to respond to me. Please... grant me "Death."
**Court Scholar:** Cerces above... Does this count as thinking till the moment I die?

> *(Trailblazer)* She seems... free.
> *(Trailblazer)* I can't imagine what it'd feel like to roam around this void for centuries...
> **Castorice:** I have seen too many farewells... Her distress is quite commonplace. There are those who will end their lives over what seems to be the smallest of worries.
> **Castorice:** For some people... This power that I possess is a "blessing" rather than a curse.
> **Castorice:** ...But I never managed to convince myself of that.
> **Mem:** Cas... Are you still trying to create a world without "Death"?
> **Castorice:** Yes. Because... When placed under time's vast scales, there is no worry in this world that cannot be solved.
> *(Trailblazer)* You should chat with Dan Heng about this.
> *(Trailblazer)* I can't agree with you on this.
> **Castorice:** This is a wish that I've held for almost a millennium. My mind might have changed once upon a time if people managed to strike up a friendship with me...
> **Castorice:** But I've never been more firm in my conviction, now that I've learned about your near-death state — It is my duty to wipe "Death" from the face of this world.

They seem to be fallen who lived during Era Chrysea. The remains left by these spirits seem to have a connection with alchemy and Thanatos.)*

*(Listen to the souls remains)*
**"Amunet":** Then, you granted him death?
**"Amunet":** That isn't like you... You would have refused even if I commanded you to do so.
**"Castorice":** I... don't know.
**"Castorice":** I did it out of sympathy, believing that I wouldn't be racked with agony, but... I am still in a state of turmoil.
**"Amunet":** Sympathy? Sympathy for that youth who triumphed in every battle?
**"Castorice":** That young man was favored by Talanton, who taught him a move that will defeat any mortal enemy with a single blow and emerge victorious every time.
**"Castorice":** He could rely on that move to always achieve victory during Era Chrysea. However, now that death has descended upon the mortal realm, that divine swordplay also means he can send others to death at will. In other words...
**"Amunet":** You feel that he's similar... to you?
**"Castorice":** Yes. Since he can never be inferior to others in martial combat, death would be the inevitable outcome every time he strikes.
**"Castorice":** That's why he approached me and pleaded... Pleaded for me to put an end to all of this.
**"Amunet":** You are perhaps feeling lost from making your peace with "Death" in so abrupt a manner.
**"Castorice":** I... have not made peace with death.
**"Castorice":** I've seen the people of Aidonia stand tall and proud in the face of death. Even before the fated finale arrived, they were already content with the lives that they'd lived...
**"Castorice":** They had such proud expressions... Expressions that made me believe "Death" would be an insult to these mortals if the concept is truly a power that a god had carelessly cast down upon the world.
**"Castorice":** Perhaps death's authority was never meant to exist in this world... just like how I shouldn't exist.
**"Amunet":** I won't refute your beliefs for now, but would you be interested in listening to a story of mine?
**"Amunet":** It's a fairy tale from foreign lands, told by the person who brought you to me in that ice field long ago...
**"Amunet":** ...A tale told by the Queen of Styxia.
**Mem:** What? W—W—W—Wait a minute...
**Mem:** That means... The girl born in the dragon's embrace... The personification of "Death" these souls talked about... That's you? Cas???
**Castorice:** I've already had an inkling for a while now... But I honestly have no memories of Styxia...
**Castorice:** My memories only begin... After Amunet brought me to Aidonia.

**"Queen of Styxia":** We had been inebriated in Phagousa's feast for too long before we began to seek divine-bestowed "Death," so that we could be freed from the whims of fate... However, that brought doom upon us.
**"Queen of Styxia":** Instead of summoning a supreme god, we encountered a monster that cut off the River of Souls. That detestable dragon Pollux denied all the dead from the gates of the nether realm.
**"Queen of Styxia":** Unable to rest in peace, these departed spirits engulfed Styxia like a roaring tide. As a result, my city and its people no longer exist...
**"Queen of Styxia":** That child is the only person whom I managed to save. Born within the evil dragon's abdomen, she has possessed the talent of "Death" since her birth. She must have been granted to us by the gods as a chance for us to atone for our sins...
**"Queen of Styxia":** Please, you must nurture her to adulthood... and prevent her from experiencing all of the suffering that we went through.
**"Amunet":** ...I promise that I will care for her as a mother, that I will protect her from the evils of "Death."
**"Queen of Styxia":** By Phagousa above, thank you for your kindness...
**"Queen of Styxia":** As her mother, please bless this child with a name... Before I am swept away by the River of Souls, please let me know how I should address her in my next life.
**"Amunet":** ...
**"Amunet":** Like how a transient raven butterfly, a fleeting papilio castor, perches on the twig of "Death"...
**"Amunet":** "Castorice"... That shall be her name.

### Move Forward with Thanatos

**Thanatos:** Aging is an unbreakable law of nature laid upon humankind, created at the same time as "Death." When the living die, their souls will meet Thanatos, who would determine where every soul goes...
**Thanatos:** If the Reaper raises their left hand, the soul is granted death and allowed to enter the nether realm to await reincarnation. If they raise their right hand, the soul is rejected by the nether realm and returned to the mortal realm to continue walking the earth.

**Thanatos:** Yes. "Death" was originally a pair of hands, but they were split in twain when we reached the mortal realm, never to touch each other ever again...

**Thanatos:** Please... don't forget me.
**Castorice:** ...I promise you.
**Castorice:** When we meet in the next world... Even if I have endured another reincarnation...
**Castorice:** ...Even if we do not know each other's names...
**Castorice:** ...I will still hold you tightly in my arms, just like what I did when we were born.
**Thanatos:** Castorice... Castorice...
**Thanatos:** You're as beautiful... as a fleeting butterfly.

---

*(Cutscene begins)*
**Castorice:** I will bring rest to Death.
**Castorice:** And from now on... stagnant life will once again flow.
*(Cutscene ends)*

---

*(Switching to Anaxa's POV...)*
*(narrative)* Knowledge is a scholar's second sun, and it is only right that they shine its light equally upon all the fools seated before them...

---

*(The mission continues in "Citizen, Listen to Those Roaring Tides".)*
## Sub-Mission 10: Citizen, Listen to Those Roaring Tides

### Speak with Cerces

*(The citizens' assembly on the matter of "Pausing the Flame-Chase Journey" is coming to an end. Soon, everyone will witness what "vote-decided tomorrow" they will experience. Your fifteen Entry Hours are done. The last performance is about to begin.)*

*(At the same time, in Okhema)*
*(narrative)* *Loud cheers from afar*
**Cerces:** Time flies... In the blink of an eye, the fifteenth Entry Hour is already at hand.
**Cerces:** When this farce is over, your soul will be placed on the scales of life and death. Are you ready?
**Anaxa:** No need to ask so many times—of course I am.
**Cerces:** Then why do you drag your feet? Is your fear of crowds holding you back again?
**Anaxa:** Hmph, I simply find them too noisy. Paying attention to conceited fools like them would be an utter waste of my time.
**Cerces:** Such harsh words. As I see it, you are no less arrogant than they are.
**Anaxa:** I couldn't agree more.
**Cerces:** Oh? I did not expect to hear that from you. Are you not going to refute me?
**Anaxa:** I know myself better than you think I do... At the very least, better than a stowaway like you.
**Cerces:** And they say men whose ends are nigh have but kind words.
**Cerces:** Well that is exactly why I chose you.
**Anaxa:** Hmph, should I thank you for the compliment?
**Cerces:** The hour of the trial has come. Do you have an answer for me? And... how will you prove the veracity of your argument?
**Anaxa:** I suppose there is no point in putting this off any longer. Do you recall where we stopped the last time?
**Cerces:** You were saying that Amphoreus' history is cyclical...

---

**Anaxa:** Indeed. The demigods that bear the Coreflames eventually become the new Titans at the world's end...
**Anaxa:** Remember what happened on the holy mountain? Calypso, Gnaeus... As their destined ends approached, one by one, they left this world... leaving only the twins, who were fated to succeed Death.
**Anaxa:** Unfortunately, while their journey is consistent with ours — you and Calypso as well as Nikador and Gnaeus act as supporting evidence — I can only come up with a rough sketch for my theory of a cyclical history.
**Anaxa:** For all the pains I took to reach this point, I had already lost hope of ever obtaining definitive proof. After all, the past is in the past. Proving that we are the legacies of heroes of the past is a hopeless endeavor.
**Anaxa:** That is, until I met a girl named Polyxia and her sister.
**Anaxa:** If Castorice was truly sent from the past to the present for the Titan's self-serving reasons, then having lived through two cycles and with Styxia as the pivot, she will no doubt be the link to connect all of this together.
**Anaxa:** If the twins of Death can bridge two cycles to reunite at the shores of the Sea of Souls... then my hypothesis will no longer be just a hypothesis, and you, too, will get the answer to your question.

---

**Cerces:** That is all well and good, but how can you be so sure? If there is even the slightest error in your hypothesis, the girl will be shattered.
**Anaxa:** Hmph, it matters not. The most important quality a scholar must possess is foolhardiness. If you are overly cautious, then you'll never have the gumption to make the first step, and change will never come.
**Anaxa:** Besides, I, Anaxagoras, am destined to overturn this farcical world.
**Anaxa:** Come, let us go and teach those pathetic worms one final lesson.

---

### Head to the Demigod Council and Witness the Results of the Citizens' Assembly

*(Approach the top of the stair)*
**Anaxa:** Phainon... So, they are leaving the final debate to him.

*(Approach the marked location)*
**Caenis:** Let those Chrysos Heirs who started this terrible war hear you!
**Caenis:** Let them hear the voices of the people!
*(narrative)* *fanatic cheering*
**Phainon:** ...
**Anaxa:** As always, that woman knows how to stir up a crowd.
**Phainon:** Professor Anaxa...? I thought you'd pass on this.
**Anaxa:** Everyone must cast their vote, or this assembly would serve no purpose.
**Phainon:** Prof... Do you truly think it's wise to entrust the fate of the world to an assembly like this?
**Anaxa:** Okhema has stood strong under this system for a thousand years. It stands to reason that there is purpose in its existence.
**Anaxa:** But that is not what you're asking, is it? What you really want to know is: Is Aglaea making the right decision in entrusting the fate of the world to you?
**Phainon:** ...Nothing ever escapes you.
**Anaxa:** I didn't spend all those years teaching you for nothing. Most people only take four years to graduate, if I recall.
**Phainon:** Haha... To tell the truth, I feel like I'm on my way to an execution chamber instead of a podium.
**Phainon:** So... do you have an answer to that question?
**Anaxa:** No, I don't.
**Phainon:** I thought so...
**Anaxa:** Because that question is not yours to ask in the first place.
**Anaxa:** Is "Aglaea entrusting the world to you" the right decision? Don't make me laugh. That is a question that woman must answer herself. Why are you fretting about this on her behalf? Or have you been seeing yourself as her pawn all this time?
**Anaxa:** Listen up, I'll say this only once. Flip your perspective around and ask yourself this instead, "What should **I** do to ensure that the world doesn't stray from the correct path?" That is what you should be thinking about.
**Anaxa:** You still have time, so give it some thought. Oh, and don't bother telling me the answer.
**Anaxa:** Tell it to those begging for salvation from the gods.
**Phainon:** Got it.
**Phainon:** Thank you, Professor Anaxa.
**Anaxa:** That's Anaxagoras to you.
**Phainon:** Who... who'll you be voting for?
**Anaxa:** ...
**Anaxa:** That'll depend on how well you perform... May Cerces safeguard your thoughts.

### Sit Down and Listen to Phainon's Speech to the Public

*(narrative)* *crowd chatter*
**Lygus:** Elders and citizens, silence, if you please. As the Theoros, I present to you:
**Lygus:** Phainon of Aedes Elysiae, virtuous citizen of Okhema and the last speaker of the assembly.
**Phainon:** Much obliged, Lord Lygus.
**Arrogant Citizen:** I remember him — he's the penniless lad working for Aglaea!
**Confused Citizen:** Where's Lady Goldweaver? Why is she sending this guy to give the last speech? Is this really the end of the Flame-Chase Journey?
**Calm Citizen:** Can this dude really face down Caenis...?
*(narrative)* *crowd chatter*
**Phainon:** By Kephale above, dear citizens of Okhema...
**Phainon:** As you look upon me, so too do I see you.
**Phainon:** I see your fear and misgivings, your apprehension about the discord between the Chrysos Heirs and the Council of Elders... and your uneasiness about the future of the holy city.
**Phainon:** Yet, before you contemplate something as grand as the fate of the world, I ask that you look closer... Look, beyond the horizon illuminated by Kephale, at the crisis threatening to overwhelm us!
**Calm Citizen:** What...?
**Phainon:** Aedes Elysiae, my beloved hometown, is a village forgotten by time. It is a place with the most invigorating breeze and wheat fields that spread all the way to the sea... I once thought that there, I would enjoy a lifetime of peace and quiet with my friends and family.
**Phainon:** How I wish I could invite all of you to that paradise far away from the noise and chatter of the world! Alas, I can't...
**Phainon:** ...Because she... and all that she nurtured... all that I loved... they have long been devoured by the black tide. My beloved home... is no more.

---

**Phainon:** I will never forget the day the black tide struck. My father, Hieronymus, fought with a broken sword to his last breath to protect his child. My mother, Audata... She was ripped apart by monsters, all so I might live to stand before you here and now.
**Phainon:** Galba... He was a hunter. But in the end, those arms that once drew a bow instead embraced Piso, his beloved son, as they both breathed their last. Pythias, the teacher, risked her life to save her students, but a burning beam fell on her, crushing her...
**Phainon:** And what of Livia, her daughter? Did she make it out alive? ...No, she didn't. Livia, Piso, and so many other children... My childhood friends... They were all swallowed by the black tide and turned into monsters.
**Phainon:** They kept saying the same thing over and over... even up until the final moment I finally steeled my heart and granted them eternal peace with my sword:
**Phainon:** "Why, Phainon? Aren't we the best of friends?"
**Aglaea:** ...
**Phainon:** That is the black tide — a looming threat that can bring Okhema to its knees anytime. Caenis may have promised that, with the Three Titans of Calamity gone, Okhema will return to an Era Chrysea once more, but...
**Phainon:** Much as it pains me, I must disappoint you: While the demigod of Strife has done everything in his power to hold them back, "they" are still coming.
**Phainon:** By now, the name of that black-robed swordmaster... the Flame Reaver, has likely echoed throughout the city already. The roaring thunder is a message from the Strife demigod: The Flame Reaver has risen from death, and they hunger for the Coreflames of the gods.
**Arrogant Citizen:** Lies! If such a powerful enemy is indeed approaching, why is Okhema still a picture of peace?
**Phainon:** Exactly! The city of Okhema shows no signs of pandemonium or chaos. And why is that? I beg you, spare a moment to think about this. Are we able to stand here and hold this assembly in peace today because the danger has passed?
**Phainon:** No! This peace we enjoy is no gift of fate. We are able to stand here today only because the Chrysos Heirs have stepped up and shielded Okhema from calamity! With her golden threads, Aglaea has preserved — and continues to preserve — these idyllic days that all of you hold dear!
**Anaxa:** Heh...
**Phainon:** The Council of Elders claims that Aglaea will one day forsake the people because her divinity is eroding her body and soul. But ask yourselves this: Rather than offering her aid and support, why do they choose to watch and push Okhema away from the Flame-Chase Journey?
**Phainon:** The answer that no one can bring themselves to speak of... A truth stained in blood and buried in greed, fear, and vanity... Allow me to lay it bare before you.
**Phainon:** **It is because the Council of Elders, which bears Okhema's history and honor, along with this citizens' assembly we once took so much pride in, are now infested with vermin!**
**Angry Citizen:** You insolent whelp! How dare you—
**Phainon:** It is true that Aglaea has reached her limit. Why? Because she has been working tirelessly to weave the defenses of our holy city against the encroaching evil — all while these vermin are making up baseless accusations about her and scheming in the shadows!
**Phainon:** Not once has she ever failed Okhema, yet these vermin attempt to tear her apart, tarnishing her name with unfounded allegations! She may have been blessed by divinity, but hasn't her heart always stayed true to humanity?
**Phainon:** They attempt to mislead you and reduce this assembly to a crude standoff between gods and humans, but citizens of Okhema! Once more, I implore you to open your eyes and look at those around you!
**Phainon:** Examine them closely! Beneath all that finery, do you see the good heart of a human... or the poisonous glands of a pest?
**Phainon:** Make your judgment, then turn your gaze to the demigods whom so many of you regard as cold and merciless — do you not see the light of humanity in them? And those who wear the mask of humanity yet utter only hurtful, slanderous words — can you not see that their souls have been corrupted by power?
**Phainon:** Yet, no matter what your answer is, and no matter who your vote goes to... rest assured that you have nothing to fear.
**Phainon:** For Aglaea, Tribios, and I will remain your faithful guardians and pledge our lives to upholding your decision.
**Phainon:** And this vow will stay true until the end of our days, where it will be passed on to future Flame-Chasers...
**Phainon:** ...A steadfast and unwavering promise that shall endure through the ages!
*(narrative)* *crowd chatter*

---

*(narrative)* In an uproar, representative leaders from different cities in Okhema throw pieces of pottery into the dolium...
**Lygus:** At the present time, on the scales of Talanton, those who call for the continuation of the Flame-Chase Journey and those who demand its suspension...
**Lygus:** Each faction holds an equal share of the votes, resulting in the perfect balance.
**Anaxa:** As expected, this is the moment when the Flame-Chase Journey is the closest to its end.
**Lygus:** And now, it's your turn to vote, Lord Anaxagoras.
**Anaxa:** Is there anyone else who hasn't voted?
**Lygus:** No, you're the last. Cast your vote with care, for your decision will break this impasse.
**Anaxa:** I have long since made a decision.
**Lygus:** Very well. May you bring much-needed change to this farcical world.

### Go to the Moonlight. Castorice Is Beckoning for You

**(Trailblazer):** ...
**(Trailblazer):** (I'm... back.)
**Castorice:** At last... I knew you could do it.
**Castorice:** Your soul is still perfectly whole, as if some kind of force is trying its best to keep it together.
**Castorice:** Is it Oronyx's final blessing? Or... is it a miracle of your own?
**Castorice:** It's truly... incredible. Such a strange surge of familiarity... so hard to describe.
**Castorice:** Perhaps it is fate, once again fulfilling its own design. Or perhaps it is the laws from beyond the sky, so familiar to you, (Trailblazer), that intertwined our destinies long ago...
**Castorice:** ...
**Castorice:** ...It seems that this is a riddle which cannot be solved anytime soon.
**Castorice:** The sensation of becoming a demigod is truly wondrous. It's as if the River of Souls is winding its way through the depths of my spirit, transforming with each breath into a fine rain that nourishes the world...
**Castorice:** Do you recall that prophecy? "At the end of the sea of flowers, the souls of the living shall warm thy fingertips, and after an embrace... there shall be eternal separation."
**Castorice:** I promised you that I would do everything in my power to give it another meaning. Now, as I stand as a demigod and destiny draws to a close...
**Castorice:** I can finally end it in a way that is as beautiful as it deserves.
**Castorice:** The wish I once sought but could never grasp is now within reach. In this brand-new world, every blooming life will lift its arms in celebration, and recount the profound meaning of this very instant...
**Castorice:** Every touch, every embrace we share, will never again be an ice-cold loss...
**Castorice:** They will radiate with an intense warmth, sowing love far and wide throughout the land.

---

*(Cutscene begins)*
**Castorice:** The other shore... is very cold.
**Castorice:** May I borrow your warmth... one last time?
**Castorice:** Thank you for fulfilling this wish of mine.
**Castorice:** The place I'll be going to is what humans call the land of sorrows.
**Castorice:** But with this heartbeat, and the residual heat of this embrace...
**Castorice:** I shall turn the nether realm into a gentle place of rest.
**Castorice:** This embrace may be brief, but it's the proof that I once "lived."
*(narrative)* Farewell, then. May we... reunite in the new world.
*(Cutscene ends)*

### Listen to the Conversation Between Aglaea and Anaxa

*(The events in the citizens' assembly come to a temporary halt, and the Flame-Chase Journey will continue. Only at the very last moment can Aglaea and Anaxa finally break down the barriers between them.)*

*(Presently, in Okhema)*
**Aglaea:** That grand and absurd performance was what you've been seeking your whole life?
**Anaxa:** I gave up everything in search of truth, and it culminated in this very moment.
**Anaxa:** You, on the other hand, performed as I expected. You did not hesitate when you saw the chance to attain power. It's as if you...
**Aglaea:** ...Crave power like a fly craves rot.
**Anaxa:** Those are your words, not mine.
**Aglaea:** But what you said at the end... Did you already know what I had planned for Phainon?
**Anaxa:** I may have lost an eye, but my vision is clearer than anyone else. You've nearly exhausted your humanity. You're no different from my current state as a walking corpse.
**Aglaea:** That's true. I've long since burned to ash over the past one thousand years. Only he is capable of unleashing a raging fire that rivals our final great enemy — Aquila.
**Anaxa:** So, this is our last chance. How about we let go of the bad blood between us?
**Anaxa:** May I resonate with your Coreflame, Aglaea?
**Aglaea:** Please do.
**Anaxa:** ...
**Anaxa:** That's... impossible. To persist with your soul thinned to such an extent... You're insane.
**Aglaea:** Just as you fight to lug your corpse onward to complete your unfinished goals, I have my own reasons for doing this.
**Aglaea:** Until Phainon grows into his position as the prophesied leader, I must continue to lead everyone on the Flame-Chase Journey. I must keep inching forward, even if only as an empty shell.
**Anaxa:** I've long predicted this was the case, but I didn't think you'd already lost so much.
**Anaxa:** Heh, how ironic. We would have avoided so many misunderstandings if you and I had just spoken this candidly before.
**Anaxa:** But casting aside our ideological differences, there is one thing you and I agree on.
**Aglaea:** Phainon.
**Anaxa:** Yes. He was born to be a hero. He understands the hearts of the people, and I can confidently say he is a far better leader than you. Only he is capable of fulfilling your mission.
**Anaxa:** ...Sadly, he is a man cursed by Mnestia. But by my estimates, his horribly elaborate sense of style should not impede Era Nova.
**Aglaea:** Congratulations are in order. We've found common ground at the very end of our destined paths.
**Anaxa:** When you put it that way, it makes me want to take back what I said.
**Phainon:** Aglaea! We're back!
**Phainon:** Come welcome (Trailblazer), our great hero reborn from the ashes.
**Aglaea:** I hope he didn't hear your earlier assessment.
**Anaxa:** Hmph, it wouldn't be the first time I criticized him. A man should be aware of his own shortcomings.

**Anaxa:** Heh, she made quite a scene. I suppose it's my turn now.
**Aglaea:** I won't stop you...
**Phainon:** Professor, you...
**Anaxa:** What's with that look? You were present.
**Anaxa:** So, let's get this execution over with. We wouldn't want to bring shame to Okhema's divine laws or keep me from creating a new world.
**Phainon:** You never change, do you, Professor?
**Phainon:** I don't understand. If your theory is correct, you'll lose all memories of your past life once you become a Titan, so what's the point of becoming a god?
**Anaxa:** Of course there's a point. Even if I forget who I am, I'll still have my brilliant mind.
**Anaxa:** It goes without saying I'll find a way to retrieve memories of my past life. Besides, I still have you, don't I?
**Phainon:** I still don't really understand what you said during the assembly. Even if it were true, things reforged from memory could never be the same as the original anyway.
**Anaxa:** Then allow me to tell you right now that you're wrong.
**Anaxa:** What exactly are we? My answer is that the Chrysos Heirs are the future Titans, and vice versa. Of course, this answer only applies to us.
**Anaxa:** So, what are "we" when it comes to regular mortals?
**Anaxa:** **The very essence of the world — our souls — are tiny invisible seeds that hold records of each person's memories of the world, and "we" are the buds that sprout from these seeds to grow into great trees.**
**Phainon:** Is that what Nousporism means?
**Anaxa:** Correct. Once our physical bodies are born, we become new extensions of the world. From there, we plant our seeds into someone's memories and then scatter them into the hearts of countless people in the future.
**Anaxa:** Just as seeds are affected by their environment, so too will our appearances differ across memories. But the partial memories we carry will always be the same, because that is just how the world is.
**Anaxa:** To destroy our very existence, one must destroy the world itself. But we have someone who will still live on with his perfect memory...
**Anaxa:** That person is you, child of Kephale. This is what it means to be the Worldbearing Titan.
**Anaxa:** So, carry everything of this world and live on. Don't disappoint those most precious to you. Don't disappoint Lady Goldweaver.
**Anaxa:** And especially don't bring dishonor to my theory.
**Phainon:** ...
**Phainon:** Anaxa, I swear to you. I'll lead everyone to our reunion in the new world.
**Anaxa:** Hmph, good.
**Anaxa:** That's all I have to say. Farewell, everyone. May we meet again in the next life.
**Aglaea:** Farewell, Great Performer. May Cerces safeguard your thoughts.

> *(Trailblazer)* Salute him in the same way.

**Anaxa:** Are you satisfied, honorable Titan?
**Cerces:** Very. I hereby swear I have borne witness: Anaxagoras of the Nousporists and the Grove of Epiphany has successfully overcome the trial of Reason.
**Cerces:** Thus, I shall confer upon you these words.
**Cerces:** **"You shall transcend the peaks of purity and return to corruption and hardship."**
**Anaxa:** Oh, so that's a god's prophecy? It sounds like you just rephrased my past, present, and future.
**Anaxa:** Oh, Cerces, Cerces. Is that all you've got?
**Cerces:** Well, you've already "transcended the peaks of purity" and you were never the type to give me any leeway.
**Anaxa:** Hmph, that is true.
**Cerces:** But I do still have one question.
**Anaxa:** I knew it...
**Anaxa:** What is it? Maybe we're thinking of the same thing.
**Cerces:** As you've so boldly stated, we and the world itself live on through the memories of others.
**Cerces:** If that is the case, whose memories did the very first Nouspore sprout from?
**Anaxa:** How should I know?
**Cerces:** Oh my...
**Anaxa:** But since we are both equally curious...
**Anaxa:** We should allow the children of humanity who come after us to investigate in our stead.

### Listen to the Discussion Between Black Swan and Himeko

*(March 7th's condition seems to be getting worse. Black Swan still seems to be hiding something... What does Amphoreus mean to the Garden of Recollection? And why is it so important?)*

**Black Swan:** ...Thankfully, she's still alive.
**Himeko:** ...
**Black Swan:** Was March 7th also in this condition when she was first rescued by the Express?
**Himeko:** Her condition was very similar. It's just that at that time, the Express did not have a Memokeeper who could diagnose her condition.
**Black Swan:** But she still woke up naturally. Looking on the bright side, that might happen this time as well.
**Himeko:** When March awakened from the ice, her memories were completely wiped clean. I want to prepare for the worst and prevent something like that from happening again.
**Himeko:** Can you share with us your hypothesis?
**Black Swan:** I do not have conclusive evidence, but judging by her current condition...
**Black Swan:** **Perhaps March 7th's memories have been hijacked.**
**Himeko:** Hijacked?
**Black Swan:** Think about it, Miss Himeko. What will happen when someone loses their ability to remember?
**Himeko:** I'm not a biologist, but I hear that there's of a species of fish on Vonwacq that has the memory span of less than a second. It forgets anything that occurs in the very next instant. Perhaps something similar will happen?
**Black Swan:** Unfortunately, short-term memory is an entirely different concept from the inability to remember. Perhaps it is difficult for you to imagine...
**Black Swan:** But an individual who has lost their ability to remember turns into nothingness.
**Himeko:** Nothingness?
**Black Swan:** The belief of the Garden is that everything in this universe can be interpreted through memories. This is the driving force behind the continuance of this Path.
**Black Swan:** The past is made up of memoria while the future is a possibility that will eventually be converted to memoria. But the present... it doesn't really exist and is only an abstract expression.
**Black Swan:** Therefore, when a person is affected by the Path and loses their ability to remember... their physical form will also be affected. These ice crystals are a manifestation of that.
**Himeko:** ...Miss Black Swan, there is one thing that I haven't looked into deeply until now.
**Himeko:** You've guided us to Amphoreus but refused to tell us your true intentions. "Collecting memories"... is simply not a convincing answer.
**Black Swan:** Is Miss Himeko suspicious of me?
**Himeko:** I mean no offense. I am merely speculating. But I'd appreciate your honesty, if possible.
**Black Swan:** In other words, now is the time for me to prove my innocence...
**Black Swan:** That's not a problem, of course. The true reason behind my intentions is that Amphoreus holds special significance. For some Memokeepers of the Garden with extremist views, it... is a "perfect example."
**Himeko:** Perfect example?
**Black Swan:** A world that can only be seen through the Garden's mirror means that Memokeepers have the right to keep the world in their private collection. If those pathstriders of Remembrance were to uncover the secrets of Amphoreus first...
**Black Swan:** They might unleash their wild ambitions and remodel more worlds into a similar form.
**Himeko:** So, the reason you did everything you could to convince the Express to come to this starzone is just to uncover the secrets and foil the plans of these people?
**Black Swan:** I chose to remain quiet before because I did not want to involve you in the Garden's internal conflicts.
**Himeko:** Remodeling a world is no easy feat. These extremists likely aren't just simple pathstriders. Do they wield the powers of Emanators?
**Black Swan:** You can be even bolder with your guesses. The people that I'm talking about... They aren't just individuals. They are an organization that has already attached itself to the pinky finger of Fuli.
**Himeko:** Could they be the ones who hijacked March 7th's memories?
**Black Swan:** I cannot be certain, but at the very least, they are capable of doing so.
**Himeko:** ...
**Black Swan:** I am deeply sorry about what has happened to March 7th, but the deal that I proposed still remains a fair one. Amphoreus is still an excellent destination, and investigating further might allow us to uncover the truth behind the accident.
**Black Swan:** Miss Himeko, the wisest choice right now is for us to continue cooperating...
**Black Swan:** ...!?
**Himeko:** What's wrong?
**Black Swan:** ...
**Black Swan:** ...Let me take back a part of what I said just now, Miss Himeko.
**Black Swan:** Although we indeed struck a deal, it seems like I have involved you in some unforeseen danger... Please accept my deepest apologies.
**Himeko:** Has something changed?
**Black Swan:** ...
**Black Swan:** **It's Fuli... THEIR gaze has just swept across Amphoreus.**

---

*(narrative)* Clearly, Amphoreus poses quite the intriguing "question" — one compelling enough to push THAT genius into action...

## Sub-Mission 12: Homeward Journey, Never Look Back Upon the Path Whence You Came

### Resist the Land of the Dead's Temptation and Leave the Nether Realm

*(It is not easy for a lost soul to return to the mortal realm. The whispers of Death are sweet and alluring. If your determination wavers, you will succumb and be forever ensnared by the netherworld's grasp. Do not turn around. Look forward. Leave the nether realm and return to the mortal realm.)*

**(Trailblazer):** (A sea of flowers...?)
**(Trailblazer):** (What... is this place?)
**Castorice:** Allow me to reintroduce myself: I bore the name of Castorice while walking among the mortals, and now I stand as the demigod of Death, the living side of Thanatos...
**Castorice:** Welcome to my realm, where the west wind ends.

> *(Trailblazer)* It's a beautiful place.
> **Castorice:** Every departed soul who has arrived here has expressed the same.
> *(Trailblazer)* Mom, I made it! My character arc is finally done!
> **Castorice:** You're still as energetic as always.
> **Castorice:** Did you know? Every flower here was planted by Thanatos themself. These flowers were originally both lifeless and immortal, but in the first rain brought by the River of Souls, they were given new life, flourishing as the bearers of countless souls...
> **Castorice:** And it is my duty as the master of Death to tend to their well-being and foster their growth in this serene haven.
> **Castorice:** Similar to the structured cycle of life and death in the mortal world, these flowers too shall wither and fade, returning to the earth and then ascending to the sky with mist and tides...
> **Castorice:** Eventually, they will descend to the mortal land as gentle rain. Every soul undergoes the perpetual cycle of reincarnation, albeit in varied forms, until the world meets its end...
> **Castorice:** Yet, sadly, there's no time left for Amphoreus to wait for when the flowers bloom again.
> **Castorice:** Now, Death is the power at my fingertips. It once led the souls of the deceased here, but I... I shall open the gate of the netherworld and return you to the realm of the living...
> **Castorice:** In exchange... Please, take the Coreflame of Death with you and complete the Flame-Chase Journey. Do not let our work thus far be in vain.
> *(Trailblazer)* You have my word.
> **Castorice:** Thank you.
> *(Trailblazer)* But you're not going back...
> *(Trailblazer)* You're... not going back?
> *(Trailblazer)* But at what cost?
> **Castorice:** Rest assured, "defying death" is Thanatos' divine authority. Fortunately, your soul hasn't dissipated. This is not a resurrection, but the extradition of a dying person back to the present world.
> *(Trailblazer)* So you're not going back...
> **Castorice:** Mm, just like the other demigods, I will stay here... and embrace my destiny.
> **Castorice:** That being said, it is not easy to guide a lost soul back to the mortal realm. The whispers of Death are sweet and alluring. If your determination wavers, you will succumb and be forever ensnared by the netherworld's grasp.
> **Castorice:** Please consider this a one-time exception. I will do everything in my power for the Flame-Chase Journey...
> **Castorice:** ...And for your life, the only life I can save.
> *(Trailblazer)* I'll do my best.
> **Castorice:** I am... glad to hear that.

## Sub-Mission 13: Witch's Mirrored Reversal

### Listen to Herta Talking to Herself

*(Be grateful, Astral Express. Fortunately, Madam Herta is quite interested in Amphoreus and plans to conduct an on-the-ground investigation there. Madam Herta, could you speak with me, the Fourth Mirror, a little more?)*

**Herta:** ...Oh? How interesting.
**Herta:** I never expected a mere Memosnatcher to be actually hiding so much inside her head.
**Herta:** What's even more interesting is that the word "Amphoreus" has appeared in her subconscious.
**Sunday:** This lady... seems to be having fun. Is she usually like this?
**Welt:** ...It's hard to explain.
**Herta:** What are you two whispering about? Has it not occurred to you that I can hear everything clearly?
**Welt:** Please pardon me, Madam Herta. Would you mind sharing with us what you've discovered?
**Herta:** Hmph, the ones who need help are the Astral Express. You will have to put up with my methods, even if they are rather unconventional.
**Herta:** Let's get back to the topic. By using the Fourth Mirror, I rummaged through the brain of this Memosnatcher and learned a good deal.
**Herta:** Not only does this person know of Amphoreus, but there are also many Memokeepers who are interested in the place.
**Herta:** I searched through her consciousness and found the following terms: "The Eternal Land," "The Rejected Land," and "the Private Collection of the Garden of Recollection"...
**Welt:** It seems like Miss Black Swan is indeed hiding some things from us.
**Herta:** Since I'm not able to find any useful clues from the outside, why don't I personally visit this mysterious Amphoreus?
**Welt:** You're planning to head to Amphoreus yourself?
**Herta:** Yes, but not in the flesh, like you all foolishly did.
**Herta:** See these Herta Mirrors? With them, I can project my "data spirit entity" to any corner of the cosmos through spatial warps, without even needing to leave my room.
**Herta:** But of course, these "corners of the cosmos" must be within the coverage zone of Interastral Peace Communications. After all, this is collaborative technology.
**Sunday:** I see. You do seem like the more... "indoor" type.
**Herta:** Although that starzone is not within the network of the IPC, the Express itself can serve as a signal station. Come, give me the astral chart coordinates.
**Herta:** Then, sit back and enjoy the show.

*(Welt shares the Astral Express's coordinates with The Herta...)*

### Investigate the Strange Basin

**Herta:** It's either a projection or a fake. There doesn't seem to be much to take note of.
**Herta:** Now then... How am I going to get into Amphoreus?

---

*(Investigate the basin)*
**???: ** My deepest apologies, esteemed lady. I'm afraid that you cannot proceed further.
**Herta:** Who's there?
**???: ** Your visit, although not part of my calculations, is a pleasant surprise. It's a pity that the preparations are not yet in place to receive you with the hospitality that someone of your status deserves.
**Herta:** Are you not going to answer my question? Who are you?
**???: ** I have many identities, and I'm only going to have even more in the future. But if you just need a form of address, you can use my common alias...
**Lygus:** Lycurgus, Lygus.
**Herta:** Alright, Lygus. But you can't possibly think that I'm just asking for a name, can you?
**Lygus:** Regrettably, I do not have the authority to share more information.
**Lygus:** However, standards are always flexible. While not violating the ultimate protocol, I'm able to disclose this piece of information: As you thought, this is the entrance to Amphoreus.
**Lygus:** Now, please kindly turn back. Amphoreus is not a world that you should be involved with. Regardless of what kind of magnificent achievements you have accomplished, this place will not open its doors so easily.
**Herta:** You seem to understand me very well. Then you should know that I have no reason to listen to you.
**Lygus:** Your reaction is completely within my calculations. Despite my long years as a watcher, it seems like my assessment of human nature still remains accurate. From you, I am able to sense a beautiful desire for knowledge.
**Lygus:** But my answer remains unchanged. Madam, it is with the utmost goodwill that I suggest you return to where you came from.
**Herta:** Let's assume for a second that I'm not so well-mannered and that I insist on uncovering the secrets hidden behind you, Mr. Lygus...
**Herta:** ...How do you plan to dispel this notion of mine?
**Lygus:** ...
**Lygus:** Within the scope of the ultimate protocol, there are 178,244 logic chains at my disposal to convince you.
**Lygus:** After filtering through them, I have determined that I only require three to attain the results that I desire.
**Herta:** Oh? I'm all ears.
**Lygus:** First: For reasons that cannot be disclosed, Amphoreus is a completely isolated celestial body. Apart from me, no one else will be able to help you enter.
**Herta:** That's not a good argument.
**Lygus:** Second: If your plan is — as you've alluded to previously — to force me into cooperating by showing hostility, do know that...
**Lygus:** **Just like you, I have basked in the gaze of the Erudition.** I stake my honor and assure you that in the worst-case scenario, our mutual destruction will be inevitable.
**Herta:** ...Oh?
**Herta:** No wonder I couldn't find a backdoor when I did a scan of our surroundings. The safety protocols on you... are comparable to Screwy's. How interesting.
**Lygus:** I assume that you still wish to hear the third logic chain?
**Herta:** Of course. Why wouldn't I?
**Lygus:** Third: Even if you exhaust all your means and find a way to break through...
**Lygus:** You will surely give up once you understand the consequences of your actions. After all, throughout your long life — as much as you'd hate to admit — your conscience has played a crucial role.
**Herta:** Haha! What a brazen robot. You think that you can actually understand me through your paltry calculations?
**Herta:** But since you've put it that way, why don't you tell me what these consequences will be?
**Lygus:** It concerns the third Path that is entangled with Amphoreus — a Path that you and your friends still very much lack understanding of...
**Lygus:** **The Destruction.** So, if you ignore my warnings and persist in your ways...
**Lygus:** **You will break the shackles of a Lord Ravager, and when that happens, their fury will engulf the Cosmos.**
